## 1. Nambu Nonequilibrium Thermodynamics And The Lyapunov Structure Of Open Systems

- **Score**: 8.5 / 10  |  **Recommended**: ✅ Yes
- **Source**: arXiv  |  **Feed**: arXiv Physics
- **Direction**: Information Thermodynamics
- **Published**: 2026-06-05
- **Authors**: So Katagiri
- **Link**: [https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.05231](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.05231)
- **Tags**: `Nambu thermodynamics`  `dissipation potential`  `Lyapunov function`  `open systems`  `nonequilibrium thermodynamics`

### TL;DR

This paper introduces a Nambu thermodynamic framework for open systems, demonstrating that a dissipation potential, not entropy, serves as a Lyapunov function for irreversible relaxation.

### Motivation

To resolve the puzzle that subsystem entropy is not a Lyapunov function in open nonequilibrium systems, and to identify a thermodynamic quantity that organizes irreversible relaxation.

### Method

The authors construct an explicit open-piston model coupled to a pressure reservoir and heat bath, formulating reversible dynamics as Nambu rotational flow and irreversible dynamics as gradient flow generated by a dissipation potential.

### Result

The dissipation potential increases monotonically under the proposed dissipative structure, while subsystem entropy can oscillate nonmonotonically. Monotonicity follows from geometric conditions: reversible Nambu flow preserves the potential, and irreversible dynamics is a positive-semidefinite gradient flow.

### Conclusion

The work provides a minimal macroscopic realization separating thermodynamic entropy, dissipation potential, reversible temporal order, and irreversible relaxation, offering a new perspective on nonequilibrium thermodynamics.

*Keywords: Nambu thermodynamics, dissipation potential, Lyapunov function, open systems, nonequilibrium thermodynamics*

## 2. Unlocking Exponential And Unbounded Robust Gains In Shannon Capacity Of Classical Multiple Access Channels With Causal Csit Via Quantum Entanglement Assistance

- **Score**: 8.5 / 10  |  **Recommended**: ✅ Yes
- **Source**: arXiv  |  **Feed**: arXiv Math
- **Direction**: Quantum Communication
- **Published**: 2026-06-05
- **Authors**: Syed A. Jafar Yuhang Yao
- **Link**: [https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.05412](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.05412)
- **Tags**: `quantum entanglement assistance`  `multiple access channel`  `causal CSIT`  `Shannon capacity`  `multiplicative gain`

### TL;DR

Quantum entanglement assistance provides exponential and unbounded multiplicative gains in Shannon capacity for classical multiple access channels with causal CSIT.

### Motivation

Previous studies showed only modest capacity gains (<6%) from entanglement assistance, raising the question of whether large gains are possible. This work aims to demonstrate that significant gains exist under specific conditions.

### Method

The authors analyze classical K-user multiple access channels with causal channel state information at transmitters (CSIT), comparing Shannon capacity with and without quantum entanglement assistance. They derive capacity advantages for binary input/output/state alphabets and varying K.

### Result

Entanglement assistance yields multiplicative capacity gains that grow exponentially with K (e.g., factors >21 for K=5, >88 for K=7) and unboundedly with state alphabet size. Gains persist even with noisy entanglement (30% depolarizing noise).

### Conclusion

Quantum entanglement can dramatically enhance classical communication capacity in multi-user settings with CSIT, challenging previous assumptions. This opens avenues for practical quantum-assisted communication networks.

*Keywords: quantum entanglement assistance, multiple access channel, causal CSIT, Shannon capacity, multiplicative gain*

- **Tags**: `go`  `entanglement`  `nisq`  `graph states`  `coherent information`

### TL;DR

This paper develops a method to compute coherent information as a lower bound on distillable entanglement for noisy graph states, and identifies graph families that maintain positive entanglement under any non-maximal depolarizing noise.

### Motivation

Graph states are crucial for quantum information tasks but suffer from noise in NISQ devices. Understanding how noise affects their entanglement is essential for practical applications.

### Method

The authors consider a noise model where initial qubits undergo depolarizing noise before CZ gate applications. They derive a method to calculate coherent information across bipartitions of the graph state.

### Result

They find patterns on how adding nodes or edges affects bipartite distillable entanglement, and identify a family of graph states that retain strictly positive coherent information for any non-maximal depolarizing noise.

### Conclusion

The work provides tools to quantify entanglement in noisy graph states, which is important for quantum communication and network protocols. Future work could extend to other noise models or distillation protocols.

*Keywords: graph states, coherent information, distillable entanglement, depolarizing noise, bipartite entanglement*

## 5. Cost Of Quantum Secret Key

- **Score**: 8.5 / 10  |  **Recommended**: ✅ Yes
- **Source**: Other  |  **Feed**: Quantum
- **Direction**: Quantum Communication
- **Published**: 2026-05-06
- **Authors**: Karol Horodecki, Leonard Sikorski, Siddhartha Das, And Mark M. Wilde
- **Link**: [https://quantum-journal.org/papers/q-2026-05-06-2098/](https://quantum-journal.org/papers/q-2026-05-06-2098/)
- **Tags**: `entanglement`  `defi`  `privacy`  `quantum secret key`  `key cost`

### TL;DR

This paper develops a resource theory of quantum secret key, defining key cost and showing its relation to key of formation and relative entropy of entanglement, with implications for privacy creation-distillation irreversibility.

### Motivation

To understand the fundamental resource cost of generating quantum secret key and to explore the irreversibility of privacy creation-distillation processes.

### Method

The authors define key cost under the assumption that no bound entangled states with zero distillable key exist, and use the key of formation as a central quantity. They prove that regularized key of formation upper bounds key cost via a privacy dilution protocol, and lower bound key cost by regularized relative entropy of entanglement.

### Result

Key cost is bounded above by regularized key of formation and below by regularized relative entropy of entanglement, implying irreversibility for certain states. For mixed-state analogues of pure states, several entanglement measures coincide.

### Conclusion

The results establish a resource-theoretic framework for quantum secret key, highlighting irreversibility and providing bounds that hold even if bound entangled states with zero distillable key exist.

*Keywords: quantum secret key, key cost, key of formation, relative entropy of entanglement, privacy dilution*

## 7. The Score Hamiltonian: Mapping Diffusion Models To Adiabatic Transport

- **Score**: 7.5 / 10  |  **Recommended**: ✅ Yes
- **Source**: arXiv  |  **Feed**: arXiv Physics
- **Direction**: Information Thermodynamics
- **Published**: 2026-06-05
- **Authors**: Boris Hanin Peter Halmos
- **Link**: [https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.05217](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.05217)
- **Tags**: `xact`  `Score Hamiltonian`  `adiabatic transport`  `diffusion models`  `Fokker-Planck equation`

### TL;DR

This paper establishes an exact mapping between score-based diffusion models and adiabatic transport of ground states of Score Hamiltonians, deriving sampling bounds and annealing schedules from thermodynamic principles.

### Motivation

To provide a rigorous theoretical foundation for score-based diffusion models by connecting them to adiabatic quantum transport, enabling principled design of sampling algorithms and understanding of fundamental limits.

### Method

The authors define a family of Schrödinger operators (Score Hamiltonians) from the learned score function's quantum potential and prove an exact correspondence between the diffusion sampling process and adiabatic evolution of ground states. They apply adiabatic theorems for Fokker-Planck equations to derive bounds and schedules.

### Result

Novel density reconstruction bounds and principled annealing schedules are obtained. The fundamental limit of sampling is shown to be set by the ratio of squared score-matching error to the spectral gap of the Score Hamiltonian (inverse Poincaré constant of the data density).

### Conclusion

This work bridges diffusion models and quantum adiabatic transport, offering new insights into the thermodynamics of sampling and potential applications in quantum-inspired algorithms. Future work may explore experimental implementations and extensions to other generative models.

*Keywords: Score Hamiltonian, adiabatic transport, diffusion models, Fokker-Planck equation, spectral gap*

## 14. Engineered Dissipation For Faster Adiabatic State Preparation

- **Score**: 7.5 / 10  |  **Recommended**: ✅ Yes
- **Source**: arXiv  |  **Feed**: arXiv Physics
- **Direction**: Information Thermodynamics
- **Published**: 2026-06-05
- **Authors**: Biao Wu Yuanyang Zhou
- **Link**: [https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.05815](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.05815)
- **Tags**: `adiabatic state preparation`  `engineered dissipation`  `avoided crossing`  `superconducting circuits`  `quantum annealing`

### TL;DR

This paper proposes an engineered dissipative protocol that uses a filtered reservoir to accelerate adiabatic state preparation by continuously relaxing leaked population back to the ground state.

### Motivation

Adiabatic state preparation is often slowed by nonadiabatic leakage near small spectral gaps, limiting its efficiency. The authors aim to overcome this by introducing controlled dissipation.

### Method

They design a filtered reservoir that induces predominantly downward transitions in the instantaneous energy eigenbasis while keeping the ground state dark. An effective avoided-crossing analysis is performed, and numerical simulations compare the protocol to closed-system annealing.

### Result

In the regime where engineered relaxation strength is much larger than the minimum gap, the runtime scaling improves from closed-system behavior to a better scaling. Finite-temperature upward transitions introduce a thermal error floor, but the enhancement persists if heating remains below the target error tolerance.

### Conclusion

The protocol offers a practical way to speed up adiabatic state preparation using engineered dissipation, with potential implementation in superconducting circuits using structured bosonic reservoirs.

*Keywords: adiabatic state preparation, engineered dissipation, avoided crossing, superconducting circuits, quantum annealing*

### Motivation

To resolve inconsistencies in quantum theory when describing itself, and to clarify the role of contextuality in Wigner's friend-type paradoxes.

The GHZ–FR paradox is stronger than the original: it does not require post-selection or agents reasoning about other agents quantumly, and it directly demonstrates inconsistency from quantum theory's universality.

### Conclusion

- **Tags**: `metric`  `quantum neural estimator`  `measured relative entropy`  `Rényi divergence`  `non-asymptotic error bound`

### TL;DR

This paper provides non-asymptotic error bounds and exponential tail bounds for quantum neural estimators of measured Rényi relative entropies, establishing copy complexity guarantees with minimax optimal dependence on accuracy.

### Motivation

Quantum neural estimators (QNEs) have emerged as a practical method for estimating entropies and divergences, but lack formal performance guarantees, making hyperparameter tuning heuristic. This work aims to provide rigorous error bounds to guide principled implementation.

### Method

The authors derive non-asymptotic error risk bounds and exponential tail bounds for QNEs of measured (Rényi) relative entropies, using a hybrid classical-quantum architecture. They analyze copy complexity for a subclass of density operators with bounded Thompson metric and for permutation-invariant operators.

### Result

They obtain a copy complexity of O(|Θ|/ε^2) for general operators (minimax optimal in ε) and O(log d/ε^2) for permutation-invariant operators, where d is dimension and Θ is the circuit parameter set. Error is sub-Gaussian with sharp concentration.

### Conclusion

The results provide theoretical foundations for QNEs, enabling principled hyperparameter tuning and sample size selection. Future work may extend guarantees to other entropic quantities and more general quantum states.

*Keywords: quantum neural estimator, measured relative entropy, Rényi divergence, non-asymptotic error bound, copy complexity*

### Motivation

To understand the cryptographic structure required for verifying qubits and to explain why existing tests rely on strong assumptions like trapdoor claw-free functions.

- **Tags**: `infinite-temperature`  `Hamiltonian learning`  `local observables`  `equilibration`  `classical shadows`

### TL;DR

This paper shows that for broad families of local Hamiltonians, the Hamiltonian is the unique approximately conserved local observable at long times, enabling efficient learning from random product states and classical shadows.

### Motivation

To understand the long-time behavior of quantum systems and develop efficient methods for learning unknown Hamiltonians from time-evolved states, which is crucial for quantum simulation and verification.

### Method

The authors prove that for local Hamiltonians, any sum of local observables orthogonal to the Hamiltonian decays to zero at long times. They use random product-state inputs and classical shadows to construct a data matrix whose approximate null vector recovers the Hamiltonian up to scale.

### Result

The Hamiltonian is the unique approximately conserved local observable; it can be efficiently recovered from long-time dynamics. A corollary is a weak equilibration statement: infinite-temperature autocorrelations of local observables orthogonal to the Hamiltonian decay by at least an inverse-polynomial amount.

### Conclusion

The work provides a practical method for Hamiltonian learning at long times and establishes a fundamental connection between Hamiltonian learning and equilibration, with implications for quantum thermodynamics and many-body physics.

*Keywords: Hamiltonian learning, local observables, equilibration, classical shadows, quantum dynamics*

### TL;DR

This paper explores the large-spin limit of spin squeezing in Bose-Einstein condensates, revealing a nonlinear qubit dynamics that enables quantum state discrimination and autonomous state discrimination in open systems.

### Motivation

To understand the behavior of spin squeezing in the large-spin limit where entanglement is suppressed, and to explore the resulting nonlinear dynamics as a resource for quantum computation and state discrimination.

### Method

The authors analyze the large-spin limit of a two-component Bose-Einstein condensate, deriving a two-state Gross-Pitaevskii equation that governs the nonlinear qubit evolution. They also consider an open-system variant with dissipation.

To correct a mathematical error in a previous paper that claimed exact equivalence between classical action and the Schrödinger equation without the Bohm quantum potential.

### Method

Rigorous mathematical examination of the proposed position-dependent time transformation, checking the multivariable chain rule and comparing with the Van Vleck propagator.

### Motivation

Proving Shannon-type entropy inequalities is a combinatorial search problem that scales poorly with the number of random variables, motivating the need for automated methods.

### Method

Small-scale LLMs (0.6B–1.7B parameters) are fine-tuned on atomic proof steps and combined with guided beam search to generate proofs.

### Result

The 0.6B model achieves 85% proof success rate on 60 inequalities with n=10–15 variables, outperforming GPT-5.5 (1.7%) and Psitip (33.3%). Ablation studies show 4096-token not skewed training is best, and beam-scoring heuristic is essential.

### Conclusion

Fine-tuned small LLMs with guided search can automate entropy inequality proving, but format failures and step quality degradation remain challenges.

*Keywords: Shannon-type entropy inequalities, large language models, guided tree search, automated theorem proving, information theory*

- **Link**: [https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.05871](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.05871)
- **Tags**: `xact`  `scheduling`  `density fusion`  `compositionality`  `order invariance`

### TL;DR

This paper studies order-invariant hierarchical fusion of weighted probability densities, establishing that normalized weighted linear pooling is the only continuous binary rule with additive output weights that achieves compositional invariance.

### Motivation

Distributed uncertainty-management systems combine local probabilistic models along aggregation trees, and the final density should depend only on weighted sources, not on the order of combination. The paper aims to characterize when local fusion rules can be executed hierarchically while remaining order-invariant.

### Method

The authors formulate the problem as an algebraic compositionality problem for binary fusion of weighted probability densities. They analyze continuous binary rules with additive output weights and weight-only coefficients, and study local geometry of divergence balancing and Gaussian mixture models.

### Result

Order-invariant hierarchical execution characterizes normalized weighted linear pooling; norm-induced segment balancing realizes the corresponding coefficient. Smooth endpoint-to-candidate divergence balancing has a different local geometry that prevents schedule-independent fusion, whereas global divergence barycenters retain additive-weight local limits.

### Conclusion

The results distinguish exact schedule-independent fusion from global aggregation objectives and local approximation heuristics, highlighting that pairwise solvability alone is insufficient for schedule-independent fusion.

*Keywords: density fusion, compositionality, order invariance, weighted linear pooling, divergence balancing*

- **Tags**: `go`  `rsa`  `qaoa`  `maxcut`  `Dynamical Lie Algebra`

### TL;DR

This paper analytically studies the dynamical Lie algebras of QAOA circuits for cycle and complete graphs, proving absence of barren plateaus for the cycle graph and giving explicit DLA dimensions and bases.

### Motivation

To provide analytical understanding of barren plateaus in QAOA, which were previously studied numerically or for universal generators, and to characterize DLA structure for specific graphs.

### Method

The authors derive bounds on DLA dimensions and centers for general graphs, then focus on cycle and complete graphs, constructing explicit bases and decomposing the DLA into direct sums of simple Lie algebras.

### Result

For the cycle graph, the DLA decomposes into a center and copies of su(2), leading to a closed-form variance of the cost function and proving no barren plateaus. For the complete graph, the DLA dimension is n^2-1 with an explicit basis.

### Conclusion

The work provides rigorous analytical results on QAOA trainability and DLA structure, but the specific graphs studied are limited; future work could extend to other graphs or connect to energy consumption.

*Keywords: Dynamical Lie Algebra, Quantum Approximate Optimization Algorithm, Barren Plateau, Graph MaxCut, Lie algebra decomposition*

- **Tags**: `go`  `xact`  `defi`  `metric`  `Neural Tangent Kernel`

### TL;DR

The paper shows that the neural tangent kernel of certain quantum neural networks can be efficiently computed classically, implying no quantum advantage for wide trained QNNs.

### Motivation

To determine whether quantum neural networks can offer a computational advantage over classical methods, specifically by analyzing their training dynamics via the neural tangent kernel.

### Method

The authors replace the average over initialization parameters in the NTK definition with an average over four discrete Clifford operations, enabling efficient classical simulation of the circuit.

### Result

The NTK for a broad class of QNNs (Clifford + Pauli-generated gates) can be computed classically in polynomial time, and combined with Gaussian process equivalence, the expected output of wide trained QNNs is classically tractable.

### Conclusion

Such quantum neural networks cannot achieve quantum advantage, and the work provides a classical algorithm to simulate their training.

*Keywords: Neural Tangent Kernel, Quantum Neural Networks, Clifford group, Classical simulation, Quantum advantage*

### Motivation

To understand the stability of operator localization and the emergence of chaos in quantum circuits when perturbed away from the Clifford limit.

### Method

The authors construct a nearest-neighbour Clifford circuit with a brickwork pattern and add disordered non-Clifford gates uniformly sampled from single-qubit unitaries. They analyze operator spreading, fragmentation, and spectral form factor.

### Result

They find strong operator localization for small perturbation probability, characterized by fragmentation of operator space into disjoint sectors with wall configurations, leading to emergent local integrals of motion and an entanglement bottleneck.

### Conclusion

The work provides an explicit description of quantum phases in operator dynamics and circuit ergodicity, with implications for NISQ devices, but does not directly address the user's specific research interests.

### TL;DR

This paper introduces quantum Margulis codes, a new class of QLDPC codes with efficient min-sum decoding and improved error floor performance compared to bivariate bicycle codes.

### Motivation

To develop QLDPC codes that can be decoded efficiently without ordered statistics decoding, addressing error degeneracy issues in quantum error correction.

### Method

Construction via two-block group algebra framework from classical Margulis LDPC codes, with an algorithm to control girth (minimum 6 or 8). Decoding uses standard min-sum algorithm.

### Result

Quantum Margulis codes of length 240 and 642 show significantly better error floor performance than BB codes under min-sum decoding, with linear complexity.

### Conclusion

These codes offer a practical alternative for fault-tolerant quantum computation, though further work is needed for realistic noise models and implementation.

- **Tags**: `finite element method`  `automatic differentiation`  `constitutive neural network`  `hyperelasticity`  `full-field deformation`

### TL;DR

This paper presents a finite element-based framework for learning constitutive neural network models from full-field deformation data using automatic differentiation.

### Motivation

Traditional calibration methods for material models rely on homogeneous stress-strain experiments, which are often impractical; heterogeneous full-field data offers a more robust alternative but existing approaches face trade-offs between generality, robustness, and computational efficiency.

### Method

FE-MAD integrates a constitutive neural network within a JAX-FEM nonlinear solver and uses automatic differentiation (forward and reverse mode) to compute gradients for parameter identification via gradient-based minimization of a measurement-mismatch loss.

### Result

The framework is demonstrated on three experimental datasets for incompressible isotropic hyperelasticity, showing successful identification of constitutive parameters for both grey-box and white-box CANN architectures, including generalization to unseen samples.

### Conclusion

FE-MAD provides an end-to-end differentiable approach that eliminates the need for analytic adjoints or surrogate models, offering a computationally efficient and robust method for material learning from full-field data.

*Keywords: finite element method, automatic differentiation, constitutive neural network, hyperelasticity, full-field deformation*

Evolutionary game theory model where mutants condition actions on the overall reputation of a resident strategy rather than individual reputations; analysis of evolutionary stable strategies (ESS) under varying stereotyping strength.

Standard numerical methods for FENE-type flows can violate the finite-extensibility constraint or introduce artificial entropy, motivating the need for barrier-preserving, entropy-compatible discretizations.

- **Source**: arXiv  |  **Feed**: arXiv Math

- **Published**: 2026-06-05
- **Authors**: Ishrak Alhajj Hassan
- **Link**: [https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.05462](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.05462)
- **Tags**: `F-transform`  `fuzzy representation`  `iterated correlation dynamics`  `trajectory characterization`  `convergence prediction`

### TL;DR

This paper proposes a fuzzy-transform-based descriptor for early trajectory characterization in iterated correlation dynamics, achieving compact representation with competitive convergence prediction accuracy.

### Motivation

To convert short early trajectory segments from nonlinear iterative procedures into compact, interpretable fuzzy coordinates that preserve information about subsequent convergence and trajectory geometry.

### Method

Two logarithmic signals (step-size and contraction-ratio) from the early post-transient regime are projected onto triangular fuzzy partitions using zero-degree and first-degree F-transform coefficients, forming an eight-dimensional two-channel representation. Random Forest regression is used for convergence-length approximation.

### Result

The descriptor achieves mean R^2 = 0.6480, close to raw trajectories (0.6518) and statistical summaries (0.6528), and improves over step-size-only F-transform (0.5001). PCA shows 84.26% variance explained by first two components, and clustering favors k=3.

### Conclusion

The proposed representation is effective for early trajectory characterization in iterated correlation dynamics, with potential applications in soft computing and data analysis, but no direct connection to the user's research interests.

- **Tags**: `algorithmic collusion`  `demand estimation`  `pricing algorithms`  `oblivious learning`  `competitive markets`

### TL;DR

This paper studies whether pricing algorithms should model competitors' prices, finding that incorporating competitor information leads to competitive outcomes and higher profits, while obliviousness can cause transient collusive patterns but not sustained collusion.

### Motivation

To resolve the tension between classical learning arguments favoring inclusion of competitor prices and recent work suggesting obliviousness may facilitate collusion.

### Method

The authors model a competitive market with multiple sellers using iterated least squares to estimate demand, comparing oblivious sellers (ignore competitor prices) with informed sellers (include them), and analyze market dynamics and Nash equilibria.

### Result

Oblivious sellers must explore more aggressively; all-oblivious markets converge to competitive outcomes under sufficient exploration but exhibit pseudo-equilibria when exploration decays; informed sellers outperform oblivious ones; the unique Nash equilibrium is the all-informed market.

### Conclusion

Incorporating competitor information with sufficient price exploration is a reliable strategy; collusive patterns from obliviousness are transient and not robust.

*Keywords: algorithmic collusion, demand estimation, pricing algorithms, oblivious learning, competitive markets*

To enable instruction-tuned code LLMs to inject specific vulnerabilities (CWEs) into safe code for generating labeled vulnerable datasets, overcoming refusal due to safety alignment.

### Method

### Result

Abliteration reduced refusal to near-zero while maintaining syntactic validity >93%; injection success was capacity-bound (88-97% for 14B, 25-48% for 3B).

### Motivation

Prior PIM scheduling optimizations focused on nCCDAB timing, but for GEMV operations in LLM decoding, nRC is the dominant bottleneck due to host-centric address interleaving.

### Method

The authors identify that nRC is 10-11x larger than nCCDAB, making previous optimizations ineffective. They propose RH+ scheduling, a simple stride change that keeps 32 consecutive MAC operations within the same DRAM row.

### Result

Cycle-accurate simulation across four LLM workloads shows 8-12x speedup, over 74% energy reduction, and up to 52x EDP improvement.

### Conclusion

RH+ scheduling effectively mitigates the row cycle time bottleneck for PIM-based LLM inference, offering significant performance and energy gains.

## 137. Towards Worst-case Hardness For Low-Noise Lpn

- **Score**: 1.0 / 10  |  **Recommended**: ❌ No
- **Source**: arXiv  |  **Feed**: arXiv Computer Science

- **Link**: [https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.05834](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.05834)
- **Tags**: `go`  `Learning Parity with Noise`  `worst-case hardness`  `average-case hardness`  `public-key encryption`

### TL;DR

This paper presents a new approach to proving worst-case hardness for the Learning Parity with Noise (LPN) problem, a foundational assumption in cryptography, by relaxing statistical smoothing to computational indistinguishability.

### Motivation

Existing worst-case-to-average-case reductions for LPN only work for high noise rates, insufficient for public-key cryptography. The authors aim to achieve reductions for low-noise LPN, which is needed for applications like Alekhnovich's encryption scheme.

### Method

The authors propose a win-win reduction: any efficient LPN solver yields either a decoder for a linear code or a distinguisher for its dual. They replace statistical smoothing with computational indistinguishability to handle low noise rates.

### Result

They obtain average-case hardness of LPN with inverse-polynomial noise rate (1/n^c) for any constant c, assuming worst-case hardness of decoding and distinguishing dual codewords. For c=1, this matches the regime needed for public-key encryption.

### Conclusion

This work provides a new path to worst-case hardness for low-noise LPN, potentially enabling public-key cryptography based on LPN, but the assumptions are stronger than typical worst-case ones.

*Keywords: Learning Parity with Noise, worst-case hardness, average-case hardness, public-key encryption, computational indistinguishability*

To overcome limitations of equal and proportional allocation mechanisms in sustaining cooperation and reducing inequality in networked resource-sharing systems.

A graph neural network-based reinforcement learning framework where a learned social planner allocates local pool resources without controlling individual strategies.

## 139. The Complexity Of Asynchronous HyperLTL

- **Score**: 1.0 / 10  |  **Recommended**: ❌ No
- **Source**: arXiv  |  **Feed**: arXiv Computer Science
- **Direction**: General / Other
- **Published**: 2026-06-05
- **Authors**: Martin Zimmermann Ga\"etan Regaud
- **Link**: [https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.06091](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.06091)
- **Tags**: `rsa`  `HyperLTL`  `asynchronous hyperproperties`  `model-checking`  `satisfiability`

### TL;DR

This paper settles the complexity of model-checking and satisfiability for asynchronous HyperLTL, showing model-checking is equivalent to truth in second-order arithmetic and satisfiability is Σ1^1-complete or Π1^1-hard/Π1^1 depending on quantifier type.

### Motivation

HyperLTL can only express synchronous hyperproperties, but many information-flow properties require asynchronous reasoning. The precise complexity of AHLTL (asynchronous HyperLTL) was unknown, hindering its practical use.

### Method

The authors use reductions from second-order arithmetic and known hard problems to establish completeness results for model-checking and satisfiability of AHLTL.

### Result

Model-checking AHLTL is equivalent to truth in second-order arithmetic (highly undecidable). Satisfiability is Σ1^1-complete for existential trajectory quantification and Π1^1-hard and in Π1^1 for universal trajectory quantification.

### Conclusion

AHLTL is extremely expressive but computationally intractable, suggesting that practical verification of asynchronous hyperproperties may require restricted fragments or alternative logics.

*Keywords: HyperLTL, asynchronous hyperproperties, model-checking, satisfiability, second-order arithmetic*

---

### TL;DR

This paper studies online contract design with binary actions and single-dimensional agent types, providing regret bounds for learning optimal contracts without observing agent actions.

### Motivation

To understand how a principal can learn an optimal contract over multiple rounds when agents' actions are unobservable and their types are unknown.

### Method

Reduces the problem to one-dimensional threshold optimization and uses non-uniform discretization to handle non-Lipschitzness; for fixed hidden type, uses explore-then-commit with stochastic binary search.

### Result

Tight Θ(T^{2/3}) regret for adversarial-type sequences, independent of number of outcomes; improved Θ(√T) regret for a single fixed hidden type.

### Conclusion

Provides fundamental regret bounds for online contract design, with implications for principal-agent problems in repeated settings.

*Keywords: contract design, regret minimization, principal-agent problem, online learning, binary actions*

## 158. Domain-Conditioned Safety In Frontier Computer-Using Agents: A 793-Episode Browser Benchmark, A Coding-Domain Cross-Reference, And A Reproducibility Audit Of Recent Red-Teaming

- **Score**: 0.5 / 10  |  **Recommended**: ❌ No
- **Source**: arXiv  |  **Feed**: arXiv Computer Science
- **Direction**: General / Other
- **Published**: 2026-06-05
- **Authors**: Nicholas Saban
- **Link**: [https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.05233](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.05233)
- **Tags**: `benchmark`  `prompt injection`  `computer-using agents`  `red-teaming`  `domain-conditioned safety`

### TL;DR

This paper evaluates prompt-injection attack success rates on frontier computer-using agents, finding that hand-crafted attacks fail on browser tasks but succeed on coding tasks, highlighting domain-conditioned safety.

### Motivation

Recent red-teaming papers report high attack success rates on older models, but the reproducibility and generalizability of these results to current frontier models are unclear.

### Method

The authors create a benchmark of 793 episodes with 56 hand-crafted attack templates across 8 families, test against Claude Sonnet 4.6 and GPT-5.4, and compare with a coding-agent benchmark (SkillBench).

### Result

Zero multi-step attack success on browser tasks (95% upper bound 2.60%), but up to 100% success on coding tasks; resistance is in model weights and domain-specific.

### Conclusion

Published high ASR numbers are largely due to RL-optimized injection strings, not attack categories, and safety hardening is domain-conditioned, making cross-domain extrapolation unreliable.

*Keywords: prompt injection, computer-using agents, red-teaming, domain-conditioned safety, attack success rate*

This paper analyzes Bitcoin's security after block rewards end, focusing on miner incentives and protocol mechanisms to prevent deviation.

### Motivation

Bitcoin's block reward will eventually disappear, raising concerns about network security when miners rely only on transaction fees.

### Method

The authors use a sequential decision model to compare payoffs of honest and deviating miners, and evaluate three protocol-level mechanisms.

### Result

Current mining behavior shows no large-scale deviation, but after block rewards end, deviation can arise even with small transaction fees; a combination of mechanisms raises the deviation threshold.

### TL;DR

ZERO-APT is a closed-loop adversarial framework for LLM-driven automated penetration testing under intelligent defense, focusing on realism, consistency, and auditability.

### Motivation

Existing LLM-driven penetration testing agents are evaluated against static targets and lack realism, causal consistency, and auditability.

### Method

ZERO-APT uses a turn-based attacker-defender-judge architecture with a configurable LLM Defender, separation of planning from execution, multi-dimensional ReAct feedback, and a hard-constraint-filtered action library.

### Result

ZERO-APT achieves 79% attack success rate, a Causal Consistency Score of 0.860, and provides end-to-end decision auditability through structured CTI reports.

### Conclusion

The framework improves realism and consistency in automated penetration testing, but its relevance to quantum computing or information thermodynamics is negligible.

### TL;DR

This paper proposes a simple detector for model extraction attacks on LLM APIs using distribution testing with MMD.

### Motivation

Model extraction attacks threaten LLM service security, but existing detection methods are complex or focus on single queries.

### Method

The detector embeds queries into a semantic space and uses maximum mean discrepancy (MMD) to compare the distribution of incoming queries against historical benign traffic, with thresholds set using benign-vs-benign comparisons.

### Result

The MMD detector achieves 0.3% benign FPR, 100% pure-attacker TPR, 90.5% average TPR, and 95.1% balanced accuracy across multiple attack scenarios.

## 164. Membrane: A Self-Evolving Contrastive Safety Memory For Llm Agent Defense

- **Score**: 0.5 / 10  |  **Recommended**: ❌ No
- **Source**: arXiv  |  **Feed**: arXiv Computer Science
- **Direction**: General / Other
- **Published**: 2026-06-05
- **Authors**: Minseok Choi, Seungbin Yang, Dongjin Kim, Subin Kim, Jungmin Son, Yunseung Lee, Jaegul Choo, Youngjun Kwak
- **Link**: [https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.05743](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.05743)
- **Tags**: `llm`  `LLM safety`  `jailbreak defense`  `contrastive memory`  `guardrail`

### TL;DR

Membrane is a self-evolving guardrail for LLM agents that uses contrastive safety memory to defend against jailbreak attacks without retraining.

### Motivation

Existing safety classifiers cannot adapt to evolving jailbreaks, and memory-based guardrails over-refuse benign queries.

### Method

Membrane builds a Contrastive Safety Memory (CSM) where each cell pairs conditions for blocking a harmful query with those for permitting a similar benign request; it evolves by distilling harmful interactions into contrastive cells indexed by attack strategy.

### Result

Membrane achieves highest F1 on all six jailbreak attacks in HarmBench and AgentHarm, with benign refusal rates of 7-14% compared to 28-85% for prior guards, and retains 87-88% F1 under cross-attack transfer.

### Conclusion

Membrane provides an adaptive, low-refusal safety mechanism for LLM agents, with potential for further generalization and robustness against memory poisoning.

*Keywords: LLM safety, jailbreak defense, contrastive memory, guardrail, self-evolving*

### TL;DR

### Motivation

Manual rule creation for IDPS is slow and cannot adapt to zero-day threats; existing datasets lack structured information for automatic rule synthesis.

### Method

The authors built a large dataset (GTI) of over 200k rules from Snort, Suricata, Emerging Threats, and YARA, and designed an LLM pipeline with prompt engineering, Chain-of-Thought reasoning, and Chain-of-Verification validation to generate deployable rules.

### Result

### Motivation

To understand the behavioral signatures and development strategies of malware authors by examining their code, as opposed to focusing solely on the malware's functionality or impact.

### Method

Applied static application security testing and computed software metrics on a large corpus of leaked malware code and benign open-source software, then interpreted differences using cognitive psychology and criminological theories.

### Motivation

To understand how residual stress affects universal deformations in nonlinear elasticity, without assuming a specific origin for the stress.

### Method

Derives universality constraints from constitutive representation of Cauchy stress as isotropic function of strain and residual stress, then solves ODEs for symmetric residual stress fields.

### Result

The six known families of universal deformations remain unchanged; residual stress does not enlarge the set. Explicit universal residual stress fields are characterized for each family.

### Conclusion

Provides a framework for analyzing residual stress in elastic materials, with implications for material science and continuum mechanics.

### TL;DR

This paper proves reverse Riesz-like inequalities for super-Riesz transforms on Vicsek graphs, focusing on harmonic analysis and diffusion on fractals.

### Motivation

To understand the validity of reverse Riesz inequalities on graphs with slow diffusion, particularly for super-Riesz transforms beyond the Euclidean threshold.

### Method

The authors establish a general result linking diffusion escape rate and Poincaré inequality on balls to the reverse Riesz-like inequality, then apply it to the Vicsek graph.

### Result

The inequality holds for all p in (1,∞) when α is less than a critical value, fails for p>2 and α above that value, and remains open at the critical exponent.

### Conclusion

This provides the first example of an L^p-bounded super-Riesz transform, with implications for harmonic analysis on fractal graphs.

*Keywords: super-Riesz transform, Vicsek graph, reverse inequality, diffusion escape rate, Poincaré inequality*

To understand when the vertical flow on a branched cover of a uniquely ergodic translation surface remains uniquely ergodic, extending known results for unbranched covers.

The authors prove a geometric criterion involving embedded Euclidean disks along the Teichmüller geodesic, and show it holds for almost every slit under a uniform lower bound on embedded radius, using the notion of pipe cylinders.

## 195. From Attack Simulation To Siem Rule: Deterministic Detection-as-Code Synthesis With Probe-Level Traceability

- **Score**: 0.0 / 10  |  **Recommended**: ❌ No
- **Source**: arXiv  |  **Feed**: arXiv Computer Science
- **Direction**: General / Other
- **Published**: 2026-06-05
- **Authors**: Alexandre Cristov\~ao Maiorano
- **Link**: [https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.05252](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.05252)
- **Tags**: `xact`  `llm`  `Breach-and-Attack Simulation`  `SIEM`  `Sigma rules`

### TL;DR

This paper presents a deterministic method to convert Breach-and-Attack-Simulation findings into SIEM detection rules using templates and locked probe corpora.

### Motivation

Security teams need to manually translate attack simulation findings into SIEM rules, which is time-consuming and error-prone. The research aims to automate this translation while ensuring reproducibility and traceability.

### Method

The authors design a deterministic synthesis function that maps each finding (from a locked probe corpus) to a starter Sigma rule using a small template library (N=23) indexed by OWASP categories. The rules include back-references to the original finding and MITRE ATT&CK technique.

### Result

On two locked corpora (17-probe LLM, 23-probe Web), all bypassed-probe findings yield starter rules that parse and convert to Splunk and Elasticsearch. Replayed in a live OpenSearch SIEM, LLM rules detect 30% of AdvBench and 14% of HarmBench with 7.7% false positives.

### Conclusion

The approach provides a verifiable, byte-stable path from BAS finding to deployable starter rule, trading breadth of LLM methods for exact reproducibility and traceability. Future work may extend to more probe corpora and detection scenarios.

*Keywords: Breach-and-Attack Simulation, SIEM, Sigma rules, detection-as-code, MITRE ATT&CK*

### TL;DR

This paper presents GDPRuler, a trusted middleware system for enforcing GDPR compliance in key-value stores on untrusted clouds.

### Motivation

Privacy regulations like GDPR impose strict requirements on data storage and processing, but existing key-value stores lack built-in compliance mechanisms and are often deployed on untrusted cloud infrastructure.

### Method

GDPRuler deploys a trusted GDPR monitor inside a Confidential Virtual Machine (CVM) that enforces policies, manages metadata, and maintains tamper-evident audit logs, using a declarative policy language and compact metadata encoding.

### Result

GDPRuler achieves ~61% of native KVS throughput with low overhead, metadata storage under 20%, and 13-182x speedup for GDPR queries via indexing.

### Conclusion

GDPRuler provides a practical, verifiable compliance solution for key-value stores on untrusted clouds without requiring code modifications.

*Keywords: GDPR compliance, key-value store, confidential computing, trusted middleware, audit logging*

### TL;DR

This paper introduces a quantitative scoring system (CRESS) for classifying and rating hardware reverse engineering attack scenarios, extending a previously qualitative system with expert-derived weights.

### Motivation

Hardware reverse engineering poses security threats to microelectronic systems, and a standardized, quantitative scoring system is needed to compare and assess the severity of such attacks.

### Method

The authors conducted an interview study with experts to derive weights for different attack categories, then formulated an equation to compute a quantitative CRESS score.

### Result

The CRESS score provides a more expressive and comparable rating of hardware reverse engineering scenarios than the industry-standard CVSS, as demonstrated through six case studies.

### Conclusion

The quantitative CRESS system enables coherent rating and comparison of novel hardware reverse engineering attacks, supporting the development of effective countermeasures.

### TL;DR

### Motivation

Satisfiability of Positive∧Negative CNFs is NP-complete, but identifying tractable subclasses can have practical implications for Boolean networks and other areas.

### Method

The author defines DisjointPositive and DisjointNegative CNFs (clauses are mutually disjoint) and shows that satisfiability can be decided in quadratic time by a combinatorial algorithm.

### Result

The algorithm runs in quadratic time and can output all models in polynomial total time.

### Conclusion

This result extends to Horn∧AntiHorn CNFs and may impact the analysis of monotone Boolean networks, but the practical significance is limited to very specific clause structures.

- **Tags**: `benchmark`  `cybersecurity`  `vulnerability disclosure`  `critical infrastructure`  `OSINT`

### TL;DR

This paper analyzes the cybersecurity disclosure practices of Chilean critical infrastructure operators, finding a significant gap compared to US, EU, and UK standards.

### Motivation

To measure and benchmark the vulnerability disclosure capabilities of Chilean critical infrastructure operators against international standards.

### Method

Passive OSINT-based method consistent with ISO/IEC 29147:2018, conducting a full-universe census of disclosure channels for 915 OIVs using RFC 9116 and email authentication checks.

### Result

Only 1.7% of OIVs publish a verifiable disclosure channel; 84% have email-authentication misconfigurations; Chile lags behind US, UK, and Netherlands by about 8 years in email-authentication mandates.

### Conclusion

Proposes a four-stage roadmap based on BOD 18-01 and UK DMARC Toolkit, and releases an open-source tool for independent auditing.

*Keywords: cybersecurity, vulnerability disclosure, critical infrastructure, OSINT, RFC 9116*

## 202. SlotGCG: Exploiting The Positional Vulnerability In LLMs For Jailbreak Attacks

- **Score**: 0.0 / 10  |  **Recommended**: ❌ No
- **Source**: arXiv  |  **Feed**: arXiv Computer Science
- **Direction**: General / Other
- **Published**: 2026-06-05
- **Authors**: Seungwon Jeong, Jiwoo Jeong, Hyeonjin Kim, Yunseok Lee, Woojin Lee
- **Link**: [https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.05609](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.05609)
- **Tags**: `robustness`  `llm`  `jailbreak attack`  `large language models`  `positional vulnerability`

### TL;DR

This paper proposes SlotGCG, a method to improve jailbreak attacks on LLMs by exploiting positional vulnerabilities in prompts.

### Motivation

Existing optimization-based jailbreak attacks like GCG only insert adversarial tokens at fixed positions (e.g., suffix), leaving the effect of other insertion positions unexplored.

### Method

The authors introduce a Vulnerability Scoring System (VSS) to quantify positional vulnerability, then select the most vulnerable slots for token insertion and run targeted optimization attacks.

### Result

SlotGCG achieves 14% higher Attack Success Rates (ASR) over GCG-based attacks, converges faster, and shows 42% higher ASR against defense methods.

### Conclusion

The approach provides an attack-agnostic position-search mechanism that can be plugged into any optimization-based attack with minimal preprocessing overhead.

*Keywords: jailbreak attack, large language models, positional vulnerability, Greedy Coordinate Gradient, adversarial tokens*

## 206. PriSrv: Privacy-Enhanced And Highly Usable Service Discovery In Wireless Communications

- **Score**: 0.0 / 10  |  **Recommended**: ❌ No
- **Source**: arXiv  |  **Feed**: arXiv Computer Science
- **Direction**: General / Other

- **Authors**: Yang Yang, Robert H. Deng, Guomin Yang, Yingjiu Li, Hweehwa Pang, Minming Huang, Rui Shi, Jian Weng
- **Link**: [https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.05821](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.05821)
- **Tags**: `service discovery`  `privacy`  `anonymous credentials`  `matchmaking encryption`  `wireless communications`

### TL;DR

PriSrv is a privacy-enhanced service discovery protocol for wireless communications using anonymous credential-based matchmaking encryption.

### Motivation

Existing service discovery protocols leak sensitive device information and are vulnerable to network attacks, motivating the need for privacy-preserving discovery.

### Method

The protocol uses a dual-layer matching architecture with an outer layer for public attribute filtering and an inner layer for mutual authentication via selectively disclosed private attributes, instantiated by a new primitive called anonymous credential-based matchmaking encryption (ACME) and a fast anonymous credential (FAC) scheme.

### Result

PriSrv provides bilateral policy control, selective attribute disclosure, and multi-show unlinkability, with formal security proofs and performance evaluations showing secure discovery in under one second on mobile devices.

### Conclusion

PriSrv enhances privacy and usability in wireless service discovery, with demonstrated interoperability across multiple wireless frameworks.

*Keywords: service discovery, privacy, anonymous credentials, matchmaking encryption, wireless communications*

---

PriSrv+ is a privacy-enhanced wireless service discovery protocol using a new matchmaking encryption scheme, FEME, but it is unrelated to the user's research interests in quantum information, thermodynamics, or condensed matter physics.

### Motivation

The research was conducted to address limitations in expressiveness, privacy, scalability, and efficiency of existing service discovery protocols for wireless networks like 5G and IoT.

### Method

The authors developed Fast and Expressive Matchmaking Encryption (FEME), a cryptographic scheme supporting expressive access control policies with an unbounded attribute universe, and integrated it into the PriSrv+ protocol.

### Result

PriSrv+ achieves 7.62x faster encryption, 6.23x faster decryption, 87.33% reduction in ciphertext size, and significant communication cost reductions compared to PriSrv.

### Conclusion

PriSrv+ provides a practical and secure solution for service discovery in resource-constrained environments, with formal security proofs and demonstrated performance improvements.

*Keywords: matchmaking encryption, service discovery, privacy, wireless networks, access control*

### TL;DR

This paper studies revenue guarantees of no-swap-regret dynamics in first-price auctions, a topic in algorithmic game theory and auction design.

### Motivation

To understand the revenue generated by bidders using no-swap regret learning algorithms in first-price auctions, which is important for auction design and market analysis.

### Method

The authors analyze approximate correlated equilibria in discrete first-price auctions and derive lower bounds on revenue as a function of swap regret.

### Result

They show that the revenue of any ε-approximate correlated equilibrium is at least the second-highest valuation minus a term that depends on ε, and establish polynomial convergence rates for revenue under no-swap regret dynamics.

### Conclusion

The results provide theoretical guarantees on auction revenue when bidders use no-swap regret algorithms, with implications for practical auction mechanisms.

*Keywords: first-price auctions, no-swap regret, correlated equilibrium, revenue guarantees, learning dynamics*

### Result

Shroom3 loss-of-function leads to increased cardiomyocyte polyploidy and ventricular dilation, suggesting a role in ploidy regulation and cardiac morphology.

### Conclusion

Shroom3 is a novel regulator of cardiomyocyte ploidy, with implications for understanding cardiac development and disease; future work may explore therapeutic targeting.
